Methodology: NPR/PBS News/Marist Poll of 1,289   National Registered Voters
This NPR/PBS News/Marist Poll national survey of  1,289     registered voters was conducted
October 19th through October 20th, 2026, by the Marist Poll in partnership with NPR and PBS
News. Registered voters 18 years of age and older residing in the United States      were
contacted through a multi-mode design: By phone using live interviewers (282), by text (      740),
or online (267).       All potential respondents were screened for age     and confirmation of
registration. The t    elephone sample was randomly selected from L2’s registered voter database
of the contiguous United States. The online sample was randomly selected from aggregated
online panels whose members   had been matched by Cint to the same L2 database. In surveys
self-administered on the web, two attention check questions were asked that required the
respondent to follow explicit directions. If respondents fail any data quality attention check
question, these interviews are excluded from the sample and final analysis. Coding of any
open-  ended     responses was done by a single human coder. Survey questions for all modes of
data collection were available in English or Spanish. To ensure representativeness, data was
balanced to match current estimates for registered voters  by age, gender, income, and
race/ethnicity using L2 Voter parameters. Education was balanced to match current CPS
estimates for registered voters. Region was adjusted for turnout in similar elections. A likely
voter weight was applied to registered voters based on each respondent’s estimated
probability of voting in the November 2026 election.

Results for registered voters are statistically significant within ±3.7 percentage points. Results
for likely voters are statistically significant within ±3.8 percentage points. Margins of error for
subgroups are available upon request. The design effect for this survey is 1. 82     which has been
incorporated in the calculation of all reported margins of error. Research limitations can come
from many potential sources, and there may be unmeasured factors in this or any other public
opinion poll.

The partisan breakdown for this survey among registered voters is 3 5% Democrat, 3 2%
Republican, and     30%     independent. The partisan breakdown for this survey among likely      voters
is 34% Democrat, 3 2% Republican, and 30% independent.

Questions asked in this survey with the wording, results, and cross tabulations are included in
this packet. Some questions may be held for a later release. If a question is asked of a subset
of the sample, it is noted on the table. Tables include results for subgroups to only display
crosstabs with an acceptable sampling error. It should be noted that, although you may not see
results listed for a certain group, it does not mean interviews were not completed with those
individuals. It simply means the sample size is too small to report. The Marist Poll is an
educational program in public opinion research and funded by Marist University.
